package checkers

import (
	"go/ast"
	"go/doc/comment"
	"go/token"
	"go/types"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"unicode"

	"github.com/go-critic/go-critic/checkers/internal/astwalk"
	"github.com/go-critic/go-critic/linter"
)

func init() {
	var info linter.CheckerInfo
	info.Name = "brokenDocLink"
	info.Tags = []string{linter.DiagnosticTag, linter.ExperimentalTag}
	info.Summary = "Detects broken doc comment links"
	info.Before = `
// Foo is a sample function.
// It is documented by [Bar] which does not exist.
func Foo() int`
	info.After = `
// Foo is a sample function.
// It is documented by [Foo] which is defined in this package.
func Foo() int`

	collection.AddChecker(&info, func(ctx *linter.CheckerContext) (linter.FileWalker, error) {
		return astwalk.WalkerForDocLink(&brokenDocLinkChecker{ctx: ctx}), nil
	})
}

type brokenDocLinkChecker struct {
	astwalk.WalkHandler
	ctx *linter.CheckerContext

	file       *ast.File
	imports    map[string]*types.Package
	dotImports []*types.Package
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) EnterFile(f *ast.File) bool {
	c.file = f
	c.imports = make(map[string]*types.Package)
	c.dotImports = c.dotImports[:0]
	for _, spec := range f.Imports {
		pkg := importedPackage(c.ctx, spec)
		if pkg == nil {
			continue
		}
		if spec.Name != nil && spec.Name.Name == "." {
			c.dotImports = append(c.dotImports, pkg)
			continue
		}
		c.imports[localPackageName(c.ctx, spec)] = pkg
	}
	return true
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) VisitDocLink(decl ast.Node, doc *ast.CommentGroup) {
	parser := &comment.Parser{
		LookupPackage: func(name string) (string, bool) { return name, true },
		LookupSym:     func(_, _ string) bool { return true },
	}
	parsed := parser.Parse(doc.Text())
	for _, block := range parsed.Content {
		c.visitBlock(decl, block)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) visitBlock(decl ast.Node, block comment.Block) {
	switch block := block.(type) {
	case *comment.Paragraph:
		c.visitTexts(decl, block.Text)
	case *comment.Heading:
		c.visitTexts(decl, block.Text)
	case *comment.List:
		for _, item := range block.Items {
			for _, content := range item.Content {
				c.visitBlock(decl, content)
			}
		}
	case *comment.Code:
		// Code blocks do not contain documentation links.
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) visitTexts(decl ast.Node, texts []comment.Text) {
	for _, text := range texts {
		link, ok := text.(*comment.DocLink)
		if !ok {
			continue
		}
		c.checkDocLink(decl, link)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkDocLink(decl ast.Node, link *comment.DocLink) {
	ref := docLinkText(link)
	if !validDocLinkText(ref) {
		return
	}

	switch {
	case link.ImportPath == "" && link.Recv == "":
		// [Name] local symbol.
		c.checkLocalSymbol(decl, ref, link.Name)
	case link.ImportPath == "" && link.Recv != "":
		// [Recv.Name] local method or field.
		if !validIdentifier(link.Recv) || !validIdentifier(link.Name) {
			return
		}
		c.checkLocalMember(decl, ref, link.Recv, link.Name)
	case link.ImportPath != "" && link.Recv == "" && link.Name == "":
		// [pkg] package reference.
		if !validPackageRef(link.ImportPath) {
			return
		}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkLocalSymbol(decl ast.Node, ref, name string) {
	if c.lookupLocal(name) != nil || types.Universe.Lookup(name) != nil {
		return
	}
	c.warn(decl, ref, `unknown symbol %q in current package`, name)
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkLocalMember(decl ast.Node, ref, recv, name string) {
	obj := c.lookupLocal(recv)
	if obj == nil {
		if types.Universe.Lookup(recv) != nil {
			return
		}
		c.warn(decl, ref, `type %q not found in current package`, recv)
		return
	}
	typeName, ok := obj.(*types.TypeName)
	if !ok {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `%q is not a type`, recv)
		return
	}
	if !hasMember(typeName.Type(), name) {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `type %q has no method or field %q`, recv, name)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkPackageRef(decl ast.Node, ref, pkgRef string) {
	if types.Universe.Lookup(pkgRef) != nil || isStdlibPackage(pkgRef) {
		return
	}
	if c.resolvePackage(pkgRef) == nil {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `package %q is not imported`, pkgRef)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkQualifiedSymbol(decl ast.Node, ref, pkgRef, name string) {
	pkg := c.resolvePackage(pkgRef)
	if pkg == nil {
		if isStdlibPackage(pkgRef) {
			return
		}
		c.warn(decl, ref, `package %q is not imported`, pkgRef)
		return
	}
	if !packageHasSymbol(pkg, c.ctx.Pkg, name) {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `%q not found in package %q`, name, pkgRef)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) checkQualifiedMember(decl ast.Node, ref, pkgRef, recv, name string) {
	pkg := c.resolvePackage(pkgRef)
	if pkg == nil {
		if isStdlibPackage(pkgRef) {
			return
		}
		c.warn(decl, ref, `package %q is not imported`, pkgRef)
		return
	}
	obj := pkg.Scope().Lookup(recv)
	if obj == nil {
		if pkg == c.ctx.Pkg {
			c.warn(decl, ref, `type %q not found in current package`, recv)
		} else {
			c.warn(decl, ref, `type %q not found in package %q`, recv, pkgRef)
		}
		return
	}
	typeName, ok := obj.(*types.TypeName)
	if !ok {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `%q is not a type`, recv)
		return
	}
	if !hasMember(typeName.Type(), name) {
		c.warn(decl, ref, `type %q has no method or field %q`, recv, name)
	}
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) warn(decl ast.Node, ref, format string, args ...interface{}) {
	c.ctx.Warn(decl, "[%s]: "+format, append([]interface{}{ref}, args...)...)
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) lookupLocal(name string) types.Object {
	if obj := c.ctx.Pkg.Scope().Lookup(name); obj != nil {
		return obj
	}
	for _, pkg := range c.dotImports {
		if obj := pkg.Scope().Lookup(name); obj != nil && obj.Exported() {
			return obj
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func (c *brokenDocLinkChecker) resolvePackage(ref string) *types.Package {
	if ref == c.ctx.Pkg.Name() {
		return c.ctx.Pkg
	}
	if pkg := c.imports[ref]; pkg != nil {
		return pkg
	}
	for _, spec := range c.file.Imports {
		path, err := strconv.Unquote(spec.Path.Value)
		if err != nil {
			continue
===== rest =====
			continue
		}
		if path == ref {
			return importedPackage(c.ctx, spec)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

func packageHasSymbol(pkg, currentPkg *types.Package, name string) bool {
	obj := pkg.Scope().Lookup(name)
	if obj == nil {
		return false
	}
	if pkg != currentPkg && !obj.Exported() {
		return false
	}
	return true
}

func hasMember(typ types.Type, name string) bool {
	return hasMethod(typ, name) || hasField(typ, name)
}

func hasMethod(typ types.Type, name string) bool {
	// Directly declared methods, including methods with a pointer receiver.
	if named, ok := typ.(*types.Named); ok {
		for i := 0; i < named.NumMethods(); i++ {
			if named.Method(i).Name() == name {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	// Promoted methods, both through the value and the pointer method sets.
	if methodInSet(types.NewMethodSet(typ), name) {
		return true
	}
	if _, isPtr := typ.(*types.Pointer); !isPtr {
		return methodInSet(types.NewMethodSet(types.NewPointer(typ)), name)
	}
	return false
}

func methodInSet(methodSet *types.MethodSet, name string) bool {
	for i := 0; i < methodSet.Len(); i++ {
		if methodSet.At(i).Obj().Name() == name {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func hasField(typ types.Type, name string) bool {
	switch typ := typ.(type) {
	case *types.Named:
		return hasField(typ.Underlying(), name)
	case *types.Pointer:
		return hasField(typ.Elem(), name)
	case *types.Struct:
		for i := 0; i < typ.NumFields(); i++ {
			field := typ.Field(i)
			if field.Name() == name {
				return true
			}
			if field.Embedded() && hasField(field.Type(), name) {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

func docLinkText(link *comment.DocLink) string {
	var sb strings.Builder
	for _, text := range link.Text {
		switch text := text.(type) {
		case comment.Plain:
			sb.WriteString(string(text))
		case comment.Italic:
			sb.WriteString(string(text))
		}
	}
	return sb.String()
}

func validDocLinkText(ref string) bool {
	if ref == "" || strings.ContainsAny(ref, " \t\n\r") {
		return false
	}
	ref = strings.TrimPrefix(ref, "*")
	return ref != ""
}

func validIdentifier(name string) bool {
	return token.IsIdentifier(name)
}

func isStdlibPackage(ref string) bool {
	_, ok := comment.DefaultLookupPackage(ref)
	return ok
}

func validPackageRef(ref string) bool {
	if token.IsIdentifier(ref) {
		return true
	}
	if !strings.Contains(ref, "/") {
		return false
	}
	for _, r := range ref {
		if unicode.IsLetter(r) || unicode.IsDigit(r) {
			continue
		}
		switch r {
		case '-', '_', '.', '/', '~':
			continue
		}
		return false
	}
	return true
}

func importedPackage(ctx *linter.CheckerContext, spec *ast.ImportSpec) *types.Package {
	var obj types.Object
	if spec.Name != nil {
		obj = ctx.TypesInfo.ObjectOf(spec.Name)
	} else {
		obj = ctx.TypesInfo.Implicits[spec]
	}
	pkgName, ok := obj.(*types.PkgName)
	if !ok {
		return nil
	}
	return pkgName.Imported()
}

func localPackageName(ctx *linter.CheckerContext, spec *ast.ImportSpec) string {
	if spec.Name != nil {
		return spec.Name.Name
	}
	obj := ctx.TypesInfo.Implicits[spec]

REASONING_CONTENT
The code looks good. Let me now think about whether `hasMethod`'s direct method check on `*types.Named` handles the case where `typ` is an alias
(`*types.Alias` in Go 1.22+). In Go 1.25, type aliases are represented as `*types.Alias` (since Go 1.22, `go/types` has `Alias`). When a type alias is
used, `typeName.Type()` may return `*types.Alias` instead of the aliased type. `hasMethod` doesn't handle `*types.Alias`. Similarly `hasField`
doesn't. This could cause false positives for alias types.

Let me test: define `type A = MyType` and check `[A.M]`. `typeName.Type()` for A — in Go 1.25, is it `*types.Named` or `*types.Alias`? Let me test.

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"go/ast"
	"go/importer"
	"go/parser"
	"go/token"
	"go/types"
)

func main() {
	src := `package p
type MyType struct{ X int }
func (MyType) M() {}
type A = MyType
type B MyType
`
	fset := token.NewFileSet()
	f, _ := parser.ParseFile(fset, "p.go", src, 0)
	conf := types.Config{Importer: importer.Default()}
	info := &types.Info{Types: map[ast.Expr]types.TypeAndValue{}, Defs: map[*ast.Ident]types.Object{}, Uses: map[*ast.Ident]types.Object{}, Implicits: map[ast.Node]types.Object{}}
	pkg, _ := conf.Check("p", fset, []*ast.File{f}, info)
	for _, name := range []string{"A", "B", "MyType"} {
		tn := pkg.Scope().Lookup(name).(*types.TypeName)
		fmt.Printf("%s: Type()=%T %v; IsAlias=%v\n", name, tn.Type(), tn.Type(), tn.IsAlias())
	}
}

STEP 131 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Type aliases are `*types.Alias` in Go 1.25. My `hasMethod`/`hasField` don't handle `*types.Alias`, so `[A.M]` and `[A.X]` would be falsely reported as
missing. I should add `*types.Alias` handling: for an alias, recurse into `types.Unalias(typ)` or `typ.Underlying()`. Let me update both `hasMethod`
and `hasField` to unwrap aliases.

In `hasMethod`, add:
```go
if alias, ok := typ.(*types.Alias); ok {
    return hasMethod(types.Unalias(alias), name)
}
```
Actually, `*types.Alias` has `Rhs()` method that returns the aliased type (unaliased). `types.Unalias` recursively strips aliases. Let me use
`types.Unalias(typ)` at the start of `hasMethod` and `hasField`.

But wait — does `types.Unalias` exist? Yes, `func Unalias(t Type) Type` since Go 1.22. Since go.mod is go 1.24, it's available.

Let me update `hasMethod` and `hasField` to unwrap aliases. For `hasMethod`, direct `Named.NumMethods()` doesn't work on Alias; after `types.Unalias`,
if it's a Named, we get the named type. For pointer aliases (`type P = *T`), `Unalias` returns `*T` (pointer). Good.

For `hasField`, same.

Let me also consider `typeName.Type()` could be a `*types.Alias` whose `Unalias` is a pointer. `hasMethod(alias)` → `hasMethod(Unalias(alias))` →
handles pointer. Good.

Let me update.

I need to handle type aliases (`*types.Alias`) in member lookup. Let me update.

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"go/ast"
	"go/importer"
	"go/parser"
	"go/token"
	"go/types"
)

func main() {
	src := `package p
type Box[T any] struct{}
func F[T any]() { var x T; _ = x }
`
	fset := token.NewFileSet()
	f, _ := parser.ParseFile(fset, "p.go", src, 0)
	conf := types.Config{Importer: importer.Default()}
	info := &types.Info{Types: map[ast.Expr]types.TypeAndValue{}, Defs: map[*ast.Ident]types.Object{}, Uses: map[*ast.Ident]types.Object{}, Implicits: map[ast.Node]types.Object{}, Selections: map[*ast.SelectorExpr]*types.Selection{}}
	pkg, _ := conf.Check("p", fset, []*ast.File{f}, info)
	fn := pkg.Scope().Lookup("F").(*types.Func)
	sig := fn.Type().(*types.Signature)
	tp := sig.TypeParams().At(0)
	fmt.Println("tp:", tp)
	func() {
		defer func() { fmt.Println("panic:", recover()) }()
		ms := types.NewMethodSet(tp)
		fmt.Println("methodset len:", ms.Len())
		pt := types.NewPointer(tp)
		fmt.Println("ptr:", pt)
		ms2 := types.NewMethodSet(pt)
		fmt.Println("ptr methodset len:", ms2.Len())
	}()
}
EOF
go run .

is "*Foo". `docLinkText` reconstructs from `link.Text`. For `[*Foo]`, the parser sets `Text` via `parseText(nil, text[start+1:i], false)` where the
bracket content is `*Foo` — so `link.Text` = [Plain("*Foo")]. `docLinkText` returns "*Foo". `validDocLinkText` strips `*` → "Foo", returns true. Then

MESSAGE
I've completed the implementation. Here's a summary of what was done:

## Changes

1. **New checker `brokenDocLink`** (`checkers/brokenDocLink_checker.go`)
   - Uses `go/doc/comment` (`comment.Parser`) to parse doc comment text and extract bracket-notation `*comment.DocLink` nodes.
   - Validates each link against package type information:
     - Local symbols (`[Foo]`) looked up in current package scope + dot-imported symbols.
     - Qualified symbols (`[pkg.Foo]`) resolved through the file's imports, including renamed aliases and full import paths.
     - Method/field references (`[T.M]`, `[pkg.T.M]`) verify the type exists, is a type, and the member exists — including promoted members via
embedded fields, pointer-receiver methods, generic types, and type aliases.
   - Skips content with spaces/non-identifier characters (not a valid link).
   - Skips Go builtins (`types.Universe`) and valid stdlib package references (`comment.DefaultLookupPackage`).
   - Emits diagnostics at the documented declaration node with format `[<ref>]: <reason>` and all required message formats.

2. **astwalk extension**
   - Added `DocLinkVisitor` interface in `visitor.go`.
   - Added `WalkerForDocLink` in `walker.go`.
   - Added `docLinkWalker` in `doc_link_walker.go` that visits doc comments together with their declaration nodes.

3. **Tests** (`checkers/testdata/brokenDocLink/`)
   - `positive_tests.go`: covers all seven diagnostic messages.
   - `negative_tests.go`: covers valid local/qualified/method/field refs, dot imports, renamed imports, embedded members, builtins, stdlib refs,
pointer-style links, generic types, aliases, and non-link bracket content.
   - `types.go`: shared test types.

4. **Docs** (`docs/overview.md`) regenerated via `cmd/makedocs`.

All checker tests pass, including the full `TestCheckers` suite, `TestTags`, `TestDocs`, `TestStableList`, `go vet`, and `go build ./...`.
